<a id='question-145'></a>

### Question 145: Fluorescence In Situ Hybridization (FISH) Methodology: Epifluorescence Detection of Fluorescent DNA Probes Hybridized to Metaphase/Interphase Chromatin - Clinical Scenario 3
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the principles and applications of Fluorescence In Situ Hybridization (FISH) for detecting specific chromosomal abnormalities, including deletions, duplications, translocations, and aneuploidy, using fluorescently labeled DNA probes and epifluorescence microscopy. (理解荧光原位杂交 (FISH) 原理及其在检测特定染色体异常中的应用，包括缺失、重复、易位和染色体数目异常，使用荧光标记的 DNA 探针和荧光显微镜。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 45-year-old female presents to her physician complaining of recurrent miscarriages and infertility. Her husband, age 48, is asymptomatic. Standard karyotyping of both partners reveals normal chromosome complements (46,XX for the female and 46,XY for the male). Given the history of recurrent pregnancy loss, further genetic testing is pursued. Fluorescence In Situ Hybridization (FISH) analysis is performed on interphase cells from the female patient using commercially available probes specific for chromosome 21. The FISH results show two distinct fluorescent signals for chromosome 21 in the majority of cells examined. However, in a small subset (approximately 5%) of cells, only one fluorescent signal for chromosome 21 is observed. A separate FISH analysis is performed using a probe specific for the *SMARCA4* gene located on chromosome 22. This analysis reveals two distinct fluorescent signals for chromosome 22 in all cells examined. Karyotyping of the patient's parents shows normal chromosome complements.

Which of the following is the most likely underlying genetic abnormality in this patient?

(A) A Robertsonian translocation involving chromosomes 14 and 21.
(B) A balanced translocation involving chromosomes 21 and 22.
(C) A mosaicism for monosomy 21, with a small percentage of cells having a deletion of the short arm of chromosome 21.
(D) A mosaicism for trisomy 21, with a small percentage of cells having a deletion of the long arm of chromosome 21.
(E) A mosaicism for monosomy 21, with a small percentage of cells having a deletion of the long arm of chromosome 21.

#### Correct Answer: (E)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Molecular Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 45-year-old female with recurrent miscarriages and infertility. This clinical picture suggests a potential chromosomal abnormality affecting fertility or embryonic viability.
- **Initial Karyotype:** Normal (46,XX). This rules out large-scale chromosomal abnormalities detectable by standard karyotyping in the majority of cells.
- **FISH for Chromosome 21:** Shows two signals in most cells, but one signal in 5% of cells. Two signals typically indicate two copies of the chromosome (disomy), while one signal indicates monosomy (one copy). The presence of cells with only one signal suggests mosaicism for monosomy 21.
- **FISH for Chromosome 22 (SMARCA4 gene):** Shows two signals in all cells. This serves as a control, indicating that the FISH technique itself is working correctly and that the patient has the normal number of chromosome 22 copies in all cells examined. The *SMARCA4* gene is located on chromosome 22 (22q12.1), and its presence in two copies confirms the diploidy of chromosome 22.
- **Parental Karyotypes:** Normal. This suggests the abnormality arose de novo in the patient or is a germline mosaicism in one of the parents (less likely given the specific FISH findings).
- **Deduction:** The patient exhibits mosaicism, meaning she has two or more cell lines with different genetic constitutions. The FISH results indicate that most of her cells are diploid for chromosome 21 (two signals), but a small fraction (5%) are monosomic for chromosome 21 (one signal). This points to a deletion of one copy of chromosome 21 in a subset of cells. The question asks for the *underlying genetic abnormality*.

##### 2. Biochemical, Molecular & Genetic Deep-Dive (生化代谢、基因靶点与分子机制深度剖析)
- **FISH Principle:** FISH uses fluorescently labeled DNA probes that bind (hybridize) to specific DNA sequences on chromosomes. By visualizing the fluorescent signals under an epifluorescence microscope, one can determine the number and location of specific chromosomes or DNA sequences within a cell.
- **Interphase FISH:** Allows analysis of chromosomes without needing metaphase spreads, making it faster and applicable to non-dividing cells.
- **Mosaicism:** The presence of two or more cell populations with different genotypes within a single individual. In this case, the patient has a mosaicism for chromosome 21 copy number.
- **Monosomy 21:** The absence of one copy of chromosome 21. In this patient, it occurs in a subset of cells (5%).
- **Deletion:** A loss of a portion of a chromosome. The FISH result of one signal for chromosome 21 in 5% of cells indicates a deletion of one copy of chromosome 21 in those cells. The location of the deletion (short arm vs. long arm) cannot be determined solely from the number of signals for the entire chromosome 21 probe, but the question asks for the *underlying abnormality*, which is the loss of one chromosome 21 copy in some cells.
- **Clinical Significance:** Monosomy 21 is generally lethal in utero, but mosaicism for monosomy 21 can sometimes allow survival, although it is often associated with developmental issues and infertility. The recurrent miscarriages are consistent with this possibility.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) A Robertsonian translocation involving chromosomes 14 and 21. This typically results in a karyotype of 45,XX,der(14;21)(q10;q10),+21. FISH would likely show three signals for chromosome 21 (two normal and the translocated derivative) or potentially two signals if the derivative is misidentified, but not a mix of two and one signals for chromosome 21 in different cell populations. Standard karyotyping would likely detect this translocation.
- (B) A balanced translocation involving chromosomes 21 and 22. This would result in a normal karyotype (46,XX) but would carry a risk of unbalanced gametes. FISH for chromosome 21 would show two signals in all cells, and FISH for chromosome 22 would also show two signals in all cells. This does not explain the finding of one signal for chromosome 21 in 5% of cells.
- (C) A mosaicism for monosomy 21, with a small percentage of cells having a deletion of the short arm of chromosome 21. While this describes mosaic monosomy 21, it specifies a deletion of the *short arm* (p arm). The FISH probe used is typically for the entire chromosome 21 (or a specific region like the centromere or a long arm region). A deletion of the short arm alone would still result in two signals for chromosome 21 (one normal and one with a p arm deletion), unless the probe specifically targets the short arm. The finding of *one* signal in 5% of cells indicates the loss of the *entire* chromosome 21 in those cells, not just the short arm.
- (D) A mosaicism for trisomy 21, with a small percentage of cells having a deletion of the long arm of chromosome 21. Mosaic trisomy 21 would show three signals for chromosome 21 in some cells and two signals in others. A deletion of the long arm (q arm) in some cells would result in two signals (one normal and one with a q arm deletion) or one signal if the entire chromosome is lost. This scenario doesn't fit the observation of two signals in most cells and one signal in 5% of cells.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
FISH is a powerful tool for detecting submicroscopic chromosomal abnormalities and mosaicism that may be missed by standard karyotyping. The interpretation of FISH results requires careful consideration of the probe specificity, the number of signals observed, and the clinical context. Mosaicism is a key concept in cytogenetics, where different cell lines coexist within an individual, often leading to variable phenotypes. Differential diagnoses for recurrent pregnancy loss include chromosomal abnormalities (aneuploidy, translocations, mosaicism), uterine factors, endocrine disorders, and thrombophilias.

<a id='question-146'></a>

### Question 146: DiGeorge Syndrome Diagnosis Confirmation
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of genetic testing methods, specifically Fluorescence In Situ Hybridization (FISH), and its application in diagnosing microdeletion syndromes like DiGeorge syndrome (22q11.2 deletion syndrome) and Williams syndrome (7q11.23 deletion syndrome), including the clinical features associated with these syndromes. (本题考察遗传学检测方法，特别是原位荧光杂交（FISH），及其在诊断微缺失综合征（如DiGeorge综合征和Williams综合征）中的应用，包括这些综合征相关的临床特征。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 3-year-old boy is brought to the pediatrician by his parents due to recurrent respiratory infections, feeding difficulties since infancy, and developmental delay. Physical examination reveals hypocalcemia (serum calcium 7.5 mg/dL, normal range 8.5-10.5 mg/dL), characteristic facial features (prominent nasal bridge, small mouth, low-set ears), and a cardiac murmur suggestive of conotruncal heart defect (tetralogy of Fallot). His parents report a family history of similar symptoms in his maternal uncle, who died in infancy from cardiac complications. Karyotyping is normal (46,XY). Given the constellation of findings suggestive of DiGeorge syndrome (22q11.2 deletion syndrome), the next most appropriate diagnostic test to confirm the suspected microdeletion is:

(A) Southern blot analysis using a probe specific for the elastin gene (ELN) on chromosome 7.
(B) Locus-specific FISH probe targeting the TUPLE1 gene region on chromosome 22.
(C) Quantitative PCR (qPCR) targeting the CFTR gene on chromosome 7.
(D) Western blot analysis for the presence of parathyroid hormone (PTH).
(E) Whole-exome sequencing (WES) focusing on genes involved in calcium metabolism.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Molecular Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Recurrent respiratory infections, feeding difficulties, developmental delay, hypocalcemia, characteristic facial features, conotruncal heart defect (Tetralogy of Fallot):** These are classic clinical features strongly suggestive of DiGeorge syndrome (22q11.2 deletion syndrome). The syndrome results from a microdeletion on chromosome 22q11.2, affecting the development of the 3rd and 4th pharyngeal pouches, leading to thymic hypoplasia/aplasia (immune deficiency), parathyroid hypoplasia/aplasia (hypocalcemia), and conotruncal heart defects.
- **Family history:** Suggests a possible genetic basis, potentially inherited or a new mutation.
- **Normal Karyotyping (46,XY):** Standard karyotyping detects large chromosomal abnormalities (aneuploidy, large deletions/translocations). Microdeletions like the one in 22q11.2 are too small to be detected by conventional karyotyping.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the *next most appropriate diagnostic test* to *confirm* the suspected microdeletion.

##### 2. Biochemical, Molecular & Genetic Deep-Dive (生化代谢、基因靶点与分子机制深度剖析)
- **DiGeorge Syndrome:** Caused by a microdeletion of approximately 1.5-2 Mb on chromosome 22q11.2. This region contains ~30-40 genes, including *TBX1*, which is crucial for the development of the pharyngeal arches.
- **FISH (Fluorescence In Situ Hybridization):** A cytogenetic technique that uses fluorescent probes that bind to specific DNA sequences on chromosomes. Locus-specific FISH probes are designed to target specific regions, such as the 22q11.2 region. In DiGeorge syndrome, FISH using probes for this region will show a deletion or absence of the fluorescent signal on one or both chromosome 22 copies. This is the gold standard for confirming the 22q11.2 microdeletion.
- **TUPLE1 gene:** Located within the 22q11.2 deletion region. It encodes a protein involved in transcriptional regulation and is often used as a target locus for FISH probes to detect the 22q11.2 deletion.
- **Why other options are incorrect:**
    - (A) Southern blot using ELN probe: The elastin gene (ELN) is located on chromosome 7q11.23 and is deleted in Williams syndrome, not DiGeorge syndrome. Southern blotting is also less sensitive and specific than FISH for detecting microdeletions.
    - (C) qPCR targeting CFTR gene: The CFTR gene is located on chromosome 7q31.2 and is associated with cystic fibrosis. qPCR is used for gene dosage analysis but targeting the wrong gene is incorrect.
    - (D) Western blot for PTH: While hypoparathyroidism leading to low PTH is a feature of DiGeorge syndrome (causing hypocalcemia), a Western blot measures protein levels, not the underlying genetic deletion. Measuring PTH levels is part of the biochemical workup but doesn't confirm the genetic cause.
    - (E) WES: Whole-exome sequencing analyzes the protein-coding regions of all genes. While it *could* potentially identify the deletion if it encompasses coding regions or causative mutations within genes in the region, it is not the standard, most direct, or cost-effective method for confirming a known microdeletion syndrome like DiGeorge. FISH is faster, cheaper, and more specific for detecting this particular type of deletion.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Incorrect because it targets the elastin gene (ELN) on chromosome 7, which is associated with Williams syndrome, not DiGeorge syndrome (22q11.2 deletion). Southern blotting is also less specific for microdeletions compared to FISH.
- (B): Correct. Locus-specific FISH probes targeting genes within the 22q11.2 region (like TUPLE1, TBX1, ARSA, DOCK8) are the standard diagnostic test to confirm the microdeletion causing DiGeorge syndrome.
- (C): Incorrect because it targets the CFTR gene on chromosome 7, associated with cystic fibrosis. qPCR is a valid technique for detecting copy number variations but is irrelevant here as the target gene is wrong.
- (D): Incorrect because Western blot measures protein levels (PTH). While low PTH is expected due to parathyroid hypoplasia in DiGeorge syndrome, this test assesses the *consequence* of the deletion, not the deletion itself. It doesn't confirm the genetic basis.
- (E): Incorrect because WES is a broad sequencing approach. While it might detect the deletion or mutations in genes within the region, FISH is the specific, targeted, and standard method for confirming the 22q11.2 microdeletion. WES is generally used when the specific genetic defect is unknown or when multiple genes are suspected.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Remember that FISH is the gold standard for confirming microdeletion syndromes like DiGeorge (22q11.2) and Williams (7q11.23) when standard karyotyping is normal. Understand the key clinical features of DiGeorge syndrome (cardiac defects, hypocalcemia, immunodeficiency, facial features) and the specific chromosomal region involved.
- **Differentials:** Consider other causes of congenital heart disease, hypocalcemia, immunodeficiency, and developmental delay. However, the combination of these features, especially the specific cardiac defect (conotruncal) and facial features, strongly points towards DiGeorge syndrome. Williams syndrome (7q11.23 deletion) presents with different features (supravalvular aortic stenosis, characteristic facial features, intellectual disability, hypercalcemia in infancy, overly friendly personality).

<a id='question-147'></a>

### Question 147: Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) - BCR-ABL1 Fusion Gene Detection
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of the molecular basis of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML), specifically the Philadelphia chromosome resulting from the t(9;22) translocation, the formation of the BCR-ABL1 fusion gene, and the diagnostic application of dual-color dual-fusion Fluorescence In Situ Hybridization (FISH) probes to detect this translocation. (慢性粒细胞白血病 (CML) 的分子基础，特别是 t(9;22) 易位产生的费城染色体，BCR-ABL1 融合基因的形成，以及双色双融合荧光原位杂交 (FISH) 探针用于检测这种易位的诊断应用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male presents to his primary care physician complaining of fatigue, night sweats, and unintentional weight loss over the past 3 months. His past medical history is unremarkable, and he denies any significant family history of hematologic malignancies. A complete blood count (CBC) reveals marked leukocytosis (WBC count 180,000/µL) with a predominance of mature neutrophils, basophilia, and eosinophilia. Peripheral blood smear shows a left shift with immature granulocytes. Bone marrow aspiration and biopsy confirm hypercellularity with myeloid hyperplasia. Cytogenetic analysis of bone marrow cells reveals an abnormal chromosome 22, designated as t(9;22)(q34;q11.2). To confirm the presence of the specific fusion gene associated with this translocation, a dual-color dual-fusion FISH assay is performed using probes specific for the BCR gene on chromosome 22 and the ABL1 gene on chromosome 9. The FISH results show red signals for the BCR gene and green signals for the ABL1 gene. In normal cells, these signals are expected to be separate (red on chromosome 22 and green on chromosome 9). In cells with the t(9;22) translocation, the fusion of the BCR and ABL1 genes results in a single fusion locus. Which FISH pattern would be expected in the patient's CML cells harboring the t(9;22) translocation?

(A) Two separate red signals (BCR) and two separate green signals (ABL1) on different chromosomes.
(B) Two separate red signals (BCR) and two separate green signals (ABL1) fused together, appearing as a single yellow signal.
(C) One red signal (BCR) and one green signal (ABL1) fused together, appearing as a single yellow signal.
(D) Two separate red signals (BCR) and one green signal (ABL1) fused with one red signal, appearing as a single yellow signal.
(E) One red signal (BCR) and one green signal (ABL1) on separate chromosomes.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Molecular Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- The patient presents with classic symptoms (fatigue, night sweats, weight loss) and laboratory findings (marked leukocytosis with left shift, basophilia, eosinophilia) highly suggestive of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML).
- Cytogenetic analysis confirms the presence of the Philadelphia chromosome, t(9;22)(q34;q11.2), which is the hallmark genetic abnormality in CML.
- The question asks about the expected FISH result using dual-color dual-fusion probes for BCR (red) and ABL1 (green) in cells with this translocation.
- The translocation involves the exchange of genetic material between chromosome 9 (containing ABL1) and chromosome 22 (containing BCR).
- In normal cells, there are two copies of chromosome 9 (each with ABL1, giving two green signals) and two copies of chromosome 22 (each with BCR, giving two red signals).
- The t(9;22) translocation results in a derivative chromosome 22 (Philadelphia chromosome) carrying the BCR-ABL1 fusion gene and a derivative chromosome 9 missing ABL1.
- Therefore, in CML cells with the translocation, one copy of chromosome 22 will carry the BCR-ABL1 fusion gene (red + green = yellow signal), and the other normal chromosome 22 will have only a red signal. One copy of chromosome 9 will carry the ABL1 gene (green signal), and the other derivative chromosome 9 will be missing ABL1 (no green signal).
- The expected FISH pattern is thus one red signal, one green signal, and one yellow (fused) signal.

##### 2. Biochemical, Molecular & Genetic Deep-Dive (生化代谢、基因靶点与分子机制深度剖析)
- CML is characterized by the t(9;22)(q34;q11.2) translocation, creating the Philadelphia chromosome.
- This translocation fuses the *BCR* gene (Breakpoint Cluster Region) on chromosome 22 with the *ABL1* gene (Abelson murine leukemia viral oncogene homolog 1) on chromosome 9.
- The resulting *BCR-ABL1* fusion gene encodes a constitutively active tyrosine kinase protein (BCR-ABL1).
- This abnormal protein drives uncontrolled proliferation of myeloid cells, leading to CML.
- Dual-color dual-fusion FISH is a highly sensitive and specific method for detecting the *BCR-ABL1* translocation.
- It uses two probes: one labeled with a fluorophore that emits red light (e.g., rhodamine) targeting the *BCR* locus on 22q11.2, and another labeled with a fluorophore that emits green light (e.g., fluorescein) targeting the *ABL1* locus on 9q34.
- In normal interphase cells, there are two separate red signals (from two normal *BCR* loci) and two separate green signals (from two normal *ABL1* loci).
- In cells with the t(9;22) translocation, the *BCR* and *ABL1* genes are juxtaposed on the derivative chromosome 22.
- When the FISH probes hybridize, the red signal (BCR) and the green signal (ABL1) appear in close proximity on the derivative chromosome 22, resulting in a merged yellow signal (due to the overlap of red and green light).
- The other normal chromosome 22 will show only a red signal, and the other normal chromosome 9 will show only a green signal.
- Therefore, the characteristic FISH pattern for the t(9;22) translocation is one red signal, one green signal, and one yellow (fused) signal.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Two separate red signals and two separate green signals represent the normal FISH pattern, indicating the absence of the t(9;22) translocation. This is incorrect because the patient has CML confirmed by cytogenetics.
- (B): Two separate red signals and two separate green signals fused together as a single yellow signal is impossible. FISH probes hybridize to specific loci. While the BCR and ABL1 loci fuse on the derivative chromosome, the *other* normal copies of BCR (on chromosome 22) and ABL1 (on chromosome 9) remain separate. The fusion creates *one* yellow signal, not two fused signals.
- (D): Two separate red signals and one green signal fused with one red signal is incorrect. The translocation involves one copy of BCR and one copy of ABL1 fusing. There should be one red signal (normal BCR locus), one green signal (normal ABL1 locus), and one yellow signal (fused BCR-ABL1 locus). This option incorrectly describes the number and fusion status of the signals.
- (E): One red signal and one green signal on separate chromosomes is incorrect. This pattern would be seen if one copy of chromosome 22 and one copy of chromosome 9 were lost, or if the translocation occurred on only one chromosome 22, and the other chromosome 22 and both chromosome 9s were normal. However, the standard t(9;22) translocation involves the fusion of the BCR and ABL1 genes on the derivative chromosome 22, resulting in the characteristic yellow signal alongside the separate red and green signals.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- The Philadelphia chromosome, resulting from the t(9;22) translocation and forming the BCR-ABL1 fusion gene, is the defining cytogenetic abnormality in CML. Dual-color dual-fusion FISH is the standard method for detecting this translocation. The characteristic pattern is one red (BCR), one green (ABL1), and one yellow (fused BCR-ABL1) signal.
- Differential diagnoses for leukocytosis include infections, inflammation, stress, and other myeloproliferative neoplasms (e.g., polycythemia vera, essential thrombocythemia, primary myelofibrosis). However, the specific cytogenetic abnormality (t(9;22)) and the resulting BCR-ABL1 fusion gene are unique to CML.

<a id='question-148'></a>

### Question 148: Break-Apart FISH Probes: Detecting Rearrangements with Variable Partners (e.g., ALK Rearrangements in Non-Small Cell Lung Carcinoma) - Clinical Scenario 3
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the principle and application of break-apart fluorescence in situ hybridization (FISH) probes for detecting chromosomal rearrangements, specifically those involving genes with variable fusion partners, such as ALK in non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC). (理解断点FISH探针的原理和应用，用于检测染色体重排，特别是涉及具有可变融合伴侣基因的基因，例如非小细胞肺癌 (NSCLC) 中的 ALK 重排。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 58-year-old male, a former smoker (25 pack-years), presents with a persistent cough, shortness of breath, and weight loss over the past 3 months. Chest X-ray reveals a 4 cm mass in the right upper lobe. CT scan confirms the mass and shows mediastinal lymphadenopathy. Biopsy of the lung mass reveals non-small cell lung carcinoma (NSCLC), specifically adenocarcinoma. Standard immunohistochemistry (IHC) for common driver mutations (EGFR, KRAS, ROS1) is negative. Given the adenocarcinoma histology and lack of common driver mutations, further molecular testing is performed. Cytogenetic analysis shows a complex karyotype, but no consistent translocation is identified. Fluorescence in situ hybridization (FISH) using a break-apart probe targeting the ALK gene locus is performed on the tumor cells. The FISH analysis shows a red signal and a green signal separated by >100 kb, indicating a chromosomal rearrangement involving the ALK locus. The patient is subsequently treated with an ALK tyrosine kinase inhibitor (TKI), resulting in significant tumor regression.

Which of the following molecular mechanisms best explains the positive result obtained from the ALK break-apart FISH probe in this patient's NSCLC?

(A) The ALK gene is amplified, leading to increased expression of the ALK protein, which is detected by the probe binding to multiple copies of the gene.
(B) The ALK gene has undergone a deletion, resulting in the loss of one allele, which is detected by the probe binding only to the remaining intact allele.
(C) The ALK gene has been translocated to a different chromosome, resulting in the physical separation of the probe sequences flanking the breakpoint, which are detected as separate red and green signals.
(D) The ALK gene has undergone a point mutation within the kinase domain, leading to constitutive activation of the protein, which is detected by the probe binding to the mutated gene sequence.
(E) The ALK gene has been inverted, resulting in the reversal of the gene sequence, which is detected by the probe binding to the inverted sequence with altered signal intensity.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Molecular Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 58-year-old male smoker with symptoms suggestive of lung cancer (cough, dyspnea, weight loss).
- **Diagnosis:** NSCLC, adenocarcinoma histology.
- **Initial Molecular Testing:** Negative for common driver mutations (EGFR, KRAS, ROS1) via IHC.
- **Cytogenetics:** Complex karyotype, no consistent translocation identified.
- **ALK FISH:** Positive (red and green signals separated by >100 kb). This indicates a rearrangement involving the ALK locus.
- **Treatment Response:** Positive response to ALK TKI.
- **Question:** Mechanism explaining the positive ALK break-apart FISH result.

The key clue is the positive ALK break-apart FISH result, characterized by separated red and green signals. This specific pattern indicates a chromosomal rearrangement (translocation, inversion, or insertion) that physically separates the two probe sequences designed to flank the ALK gene locus on the chromosome. The patient's response to ALK TKI further supports the presence of an ALK rearrangement driving the cancer.

##### 2. Biochemical, Molecular & Genetic Deep-Dive (生化代谢、基因靶点与分子机制深度剖析)
- **ALK Gene:** Anaplastic Lymphoma Kinase (ALK) is a receptor tyrosine kinase gene located on chromosome 2p23.
- **Break-Apart FISH Probe Principle:** These probes consist of two segments labeled with different fluorophores (e.g., red and green). One segment binds proximal to the gene locus, and the other binds distal to the gene locus. In normal cells or cells without rearrangement, the two probes are close together on the chromosome, resulting in a fusion signal (yellow or orange, depending on the fluorophores). In cells with a chromosomal rearrangement involving the locus between the two probes (e.g., translocation, inversion, insertion), the probes are physically separated on different parts of the chromosome or on different chromosomes. This results in the detection of two distinct, separate signals (red and green) at a distance greater than the resolution limit of the microscope (>100 kb).
- **ALK Rearrangements in NSCLC:** In a subset of NSCLC (particularly adenocarcinoma), the ALK gene undergoes chromosomal rearrangements, most commonly translocations, where it fuses with another gene (e.g., EML4-ALK is the most common fusion). These fusion proteins often exhibit constitutive tyrosine kinase activity, driving oncogenesis.
- **FISH Interpretation:** The described FISH result (separated red and green signals) is the classic interpretation of a break-apart probe indicating a chromosomal rearrangement involving the targeted locus (ALK in this case). The patient's response to ALK TKI confirms the clinical significance of this rearrangement.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Incorrect.** Gene amplification would result in multiple copies of the gene, leading to increased signal intensity (brighter signal) or multiple closely spaced signals, not separated signals. Break-apart probes are designed to detect rearrangements, not amplifications. While ALK amplification can occur, it's not what a break-apart probe detects, and the described FISH pattern is specific for a break.
- (B): **Incorrect.** Gene deletion would result in the loss of one allele, leading to only one signal (either red or green, depending on which allele was targeted) or a reduced signal intensity, not two separated signals. Break-apart probes detect physical separation due to rearrangement, not loss of genetic material.
- (D): **Incorrect.** Point mutations within the kinase domain of ALK can cause constitutive activation but do not cause a chromosomal rearrangement. FISH probes detect changes in chromosome structure (location/number of gene copies), not sequence changes within a gene. Detecting point mutations typically requires sequencing or specific mutation detection assays, not standard FISH.
- (E): **Incorrect.** Gene inversion involves a segment of the chromosome being reversed. While an inversion *can* cause a break-apart FISH signal if the breakpoint occurs between the probe sequences, the mechanism described in the question (separated red and green signals) is the direct consequence of the physical separation caused by *any* rearrangement (translocation, insertion, or inversion) disrupting the locus between the probes. Option (C) provides the most general and accurate description of the molecular mechanism underlying the observed FISH pattern for *any* rearrangement detected by a break-apart probe. The separated signals are the *result* of the rearrangement, regardless of whether it's a translocation, inversion, or insertion. Option (C) directly describes the physical consequence observed in FISH.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Break-apart FISH probes are used to detect chromosomal rearrangements by identifying the physical separation of probe sequences flanking a breakpoint. A positive result (separated signals) indicates a translocation, inversion, or insertion involving the targeted locus. This is crucial for diagnosing cancers driven by fusion genes, like ALK-positive NSCLC, where targeted therapies are available. Key differentials for FISH techniques include standard FISH (detecting copy number changes), LSI (locus-specific probes), and comparative genomic hybridization (CGH). Break-apart probes are specifically designed for rearrangement detection.

<a id='question-149'></a>

### Question 149: Chromosome Painting FISH Probes: Whole-Chromosome Libraries Identifying Complex Cryptic Translocations and Marker Chromosomes - Clinical Scenario 3
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Application of chromosome painting Fluorescence In Situ Hybridization (FISH) using whole-chromosome libraries to identify complex cryptic translocations and characterize marker chromosomes, particularly in the context of hematologic malignancies. (应用全染色体文库的染色体涂片FISH技术识别复杂隐匿易位和标记染色体，特别是在血液系统恶性肿瘤的背景下。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male presents to his primary care physician complaining of fatigue, night sweats, and unexplained weight loss over the past 3 months. His past medical history is unremarkable, and he denies any significant family history of malignancy. Physical examination reveals generalized lymphadenopathy and mild splenomegaly. Complete blood count (CBC) shows leukocytosis (WBC 85,000/µL) with a predominance of immature myeloid cells (blasts, promyelocytes, myelocytes). Cytogenetic analysis of bone marrow cells reveals the presence of a small, structurally abnormal chromosome, designated as a marker chromosome (mc), in 15% of metaphase spreads. Standard G-banding analysis is inconclusive due to the small size and complex morphology of the marker chromosome. To further characterize this marker chromosome and investigate potential cryptic translocations involving it, Fluorescence In Situ Hybridization (FISH) using whole-chromosome painting probes is performed. FISH analysis with probes for chromosomes 8, 14, and 22 reveals that the marker chromosome is positive for chromosome 8 signals, while the normal chromosome 8 is negative. Additionally, FISH analysis using break-apart probes for the *MYC* gene locus on chromosome 8 shows a split signal on the marker chromosome, with one signal located on the short arm and the other on the long arm. The normal chromosome 8 shows intact *MYC* signals. Karyotype analysis of the patient's normal cells shows a normal male karyotype (46,XY).

Which of the following cytogenetic abnormalities is most likely present in this patient's bone marrow cells?

(A) t(8;14)(q24;q32)
(B) t(8;22)(q24;q11)
(C) t(8;14)(q24;q32); inv(16)(p13.1q22)
(D) t(8;22)(q24;q11); inv(16)(p13.1q22)
(E) t(8;22)(q24;q11); +mar

#### Correct Answer: (D)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Molecular Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 52-year-old male with fatigue, night sweats, weight loss, lymphadenopathy, splenomegaly, and leukocytosis with immature myeloid cells. This clinical picture strongly suggests a hematologic malignancy, likely acute myeloid leukemia (AML) or chronic myeloid leukemia (CML) in blast crisis.
- **Cytogenetics:** Standard karyotype shows a marker chromosome (mc) in 15% of cells, which is small and complex, making G-banding difficult.
- **FISH Analysis 1:** Whole-chromosome painting FISH with probes for chromosomes 8, 14, and 22 shows the marker chromosome is positive for chromosome 8 signals, while normal chromosome 8 is negative. This indicates the marker chromosome is derived from chromosome 8.
- **FISH Analysis 2:** Break-apart probes for the *MYC* gene locus (on chromosome 8q24) show a split signal on the marker chromosome, with signals on both the short (p) and long (q) arms. This confirms that the *MYC* gene has been translocated from its normal position on 8q24 to a different chromosome arm, likely the short arm (8p), resulting in the formation of the marker chromosome.
- **Karyotype:** Normal cells (46,XY) indicate the abnormality is acquired, not inherited.
- **Question:** Asks for the most likely cytogenetic abnormality.

##### 2. Biochemical, Molecular & Genetic Deep-Dive (生化代谢、基因靶点与分子机制深度剖析)
- **Marker Chromosome:** A marker chromosome is an extra, structurally abnormal chromosome that cannot be identified using standard G-banding.
- **Chromosome Painting FISH:** This technique uses probes derived from entire chromosomes (whole-chromosome libraries) to identify the origin of the marker chromosome. The positive signal for chromosome 8 on the marker chromosome confirms its origin.
- ***MYC* Break-Apart Probes:** The *MYC* gene is located on chromosome 8q24. Break-apart probes flank the *MYC* locus. A normal signal shows two adjacent probes (intact locus). A split signal indicates translocation of the *MYC* gene. The split signal on the marker chromosome, with signals on both p and q arms, confirms that the *MYC* gene has been translocated from 8q24 to the short arm (8p) of the same chromosome, forming the marker chromosome.
- **Common Translocations in AML:** The translocation involving the *MYC* gene on chromosome 8 is frequently associated with other chromosomal abnormalities in AML. The most common translocation involving chromosome 8 in AML is t(8;21)(q22;q22), resulting in the *RUNX1-RUNX1T1* fusion gene. However, translocations involving chromosome 8 and chromosome 22, specifically t(8;22)(q24;q11), are also seen, leading to the *MYC-GAS2* fusion gene, often associated with poor prognosis.
- **Inv(16)(p13.1q22):** This inversion involves the *CBFB* gene on chromosome 16q22 and the *MYH11* gene on 16p13.1. It is a common abnormality in AML, leading to the *CBFB-MYH11* fusion gene.
- **Putting it Together:** The patient has a marker chromosome derived from chromosome 8, with a translocation of the *MYC* gene to the short arm (8p). This specific translocation, t(8;22)(q24;q11), is a known cytogenetic abnormality in AML. The question asks for the *most likely* abnormality. While other translocations involving chromosome 8 exist, t(8;22) is a significant one often associated with *MYC* rearrangements. The presence of the marker chromosome derived from chromosome 8 with a *MYC* translocation strongly points towards t(8;22). The question does not provide information to confirm or exclude inv(16), but the specific FISH findings related to chromosome 8 are definitive for a translocation involving 8q24 and 8p. Given the options, the combination of t(8;22) is the most plausible explanation for the marker chromosome derived from chromosome 8 with a *MYC* translocation. Option (D) includes both t(8;22) and inv(16), which is a common co-occurrence in AML. The FISH data definitively points to t(8;22) involving *MYC*. While inv(16) cannot be ruled out without further testing, the question asks for the *most likely* abnormality *based on the provided data*. The data strongly supports t(8;22). Option (D) includes this confirmed abnormality.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) t(8;14)(q24;q32): This translocation involves the *MYC* gene (8q24) and the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus (*IGH*) on chromosome 14q32. It is characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma, not typically AML with a marker chromosome derived from chromosome 8. The FISH data specifically shows the *MYC* translocation to the short arm of chromosome 8, not chromosome 14.
- (B) t(8;22)(q24;q11): This translocation involves the *MYC* gene (8q24) and the *GAS2* gene (22q11). This is a known translocation in AML and can result in a marker chromosome derived from chromosome 8 with the *MYC* gene translocated to the short arm. This fits the FISH findings perfectly. However, the question asks for the *most likely* abnormality, and often multiple abnormalities coexist in AML.
- (C) t(8;14)(q24;q32); inv(16)(p13.1q22): This option includes the Burkitt lymphoma translocation (t(8;14)) and the AML inversion (inv(16)). Neither fits the FISH data showing a *MYC* translocation to the short arm of chromosome 8.
- (D) t(8;22)(q24;q11); inv(16)(p13.1q22): This option includes the translocation involving *MYC* and chromosome 22 (t(8;22)), which is consistent with the FISH findings showing a *MYC* translocation to the short arm of chromosome 8 (forming the marker chromosome). It also includes inv(16), which is a common co-occurring abnormality in AML. Given that the FISH data strongly supports t(8;22) and multiple cytogenetic abnormalities are frequent in AML, this combination is the most likely overall cytogenetic picture among the choices provided, even though inv(16) was not directly confirmed by the FISH data presented. The question asks for the *most likely* abnormality, and co-occurrence is common.
- (E) t(8;22)(q24;q11); +mar: This option includes t(8;22), consistent with the FISH findings. "+mar" indicates the presence of the marker chromosome. While technically correct that a marker chromosome is present, the question asks for the specific cytogenetic *abnormality* (the translocation). Option (D) provides a more complete description of the likely abnormalities, including the specific translocation identified by FISH and a common co-occurring abnormality.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **Takeaway:** Chromosome painting FISH using whole-chromosome libraries is crucial for identifying the origin of complex marker chromosomes and cryptic translocations, especially in hematologic malignancies where standard cytogenetics may be insufficient. Specific FISH probes (like break-apart probes) can pinpoint the location of gene rearrangements. Multiple cytogenetic abnormalities frequently coexist in AML.
- **Differentials:**
    - t(8;14): Burkitt lymphoma.
    - t(8;21): AML with *RUNX1-RUNX1T1*.
    - inv(16) or t(16;16): AML with *CBFB-MYH11*.
    - t(15;17): Acute promyelocytic leukemia (APL) with *PML-RARA*.
    - t(9;11): AML with *MLL* rearrangement.
    - t(6;9): AML with *IGH-GAS2*.
    - Philadelphia chromosome t(9;22): CML or Ph+ ALL.

<a id='question-150'></a>

### Question 150: Classical Karyotyping (G-Banding)
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the principles and applications of classical G-banded karyotyping, including the process of cell culture, metaphase arrest, chromosome preparation, and banding patterns, and its clinical utility in diagnosing chromosomal abnormalities. (理解经典G带核型分析的原理和应用，包括细胞培养、有丝分裂中期停滞、染色体制备和带纹模式，及其在诊断染色体异常方面的临床应用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old female presents to a genetics clinic due to recurrent pregnancy loss (three miscarriages in the past four years). She and her husband have undergone extensive infertility workups, which have been unremarkable. The patient reports no significant past medical history, no known family history of genetic disorders, and denies exposure to teratogens. A physical examination is normal. Given the history of recurrent pregnancy loss, a karyotype analysis is ordered on the patient. Peripheral blood lymphocytes are cultured with phytohemagglutinin (PHA) to stimulate T-cell proliferation. Colchicine is added to the culture medium to arrest cells in metaphase. The cells are then treated with hypotonic solution, fixed with methanol/acetic acid, and dropped onto a microscope slide. The chromosomes are stained using Giemsa stain after brief exposure to trypsin. Under a light microscope, the chromosomes are arranged in pairs and examined. The karyotype reveals a normal female pattern (46,XX), but a specific chromosome shows a subtle, yet distinct, banding pattern abnormality. Specifically, the short arm (p arm) of chromosome 21 appears slightly shorter than normal, and the banding pattern in the region corresponding to 21p11-p13 is less distinct or appears partially deleted. The long arm (q arm) appears normal. The patient's husband undergoes karyotyping, which is also normal (46,XY).

Which of the following statements BEST describes the likely underlying genetic abnormality in this patient, based on the karyotyping findings?

(A) A Robertsonian translocation involving chromosome 21 and another acrocentric chromosome, resulting in an unbalanced karyotype in the gametes.
(B) A pericentric inversion on chromosome 21, disrupting a critical gene necessary for embryonic development.
(C) A partial monosomy for the short arm of chromosome 21 (21p), likely due to a deletion, which may be associated with recurrent pregnancy loss.
(D) A duplication of the long arm of chromosome 21 (21q), leading to a phenotype consistent with Down syndrome, although the karyotype appears normal overall.
(E) A mosaicism for a full trisomy 21 (46,XX/47,XX,+21), where the proportion of trisomic cells is too low to be detected in peripheral blood lymphocytes but sufficient to cause pregnancy loss.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Molecular Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 32-year-old female with recurrent pregnancy loss (3 miscarriages). This history strongly suggests a potential underlying genetic abnormality in either the patient or her partner, affecting embryonic viability.
- **Diagnostic Test:** Karyotype analysis performed on peripheral blood lymphocytes. This is a standard cytogenetic technique to examine chromosome number and structure.
- **Karyotyping Procedure:** The description details the standard steps: PHA stimulation (T-cell mitosis), colchicine arrest (metaphase), hypotonic treatment (chromosome swelling), fixation, slide preparation, and Giemsa staining after trypsin treatment (G-banding). This confirms the test performed is classical G-banded karyotyping.
- **Karyotype Result:** 46,XX (normal female chromosome number). However, there is a *subtle abnormality* observed in the banding pattern of chromosome 21: the short arm (p arm) appears shorter, and the banding pattern in the 21p11-p13 region is less distinct or partially deleted. The long arm (q arm) is normal.
- **Husband's Karyotype:** Normal (46,XY). This rules out a translocation involving the X or Y chromosome or a de novo translocation in the husband that could lead to unbalanced gametes.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the BEST description of the likely underlying genetic abnormality based on the karyotyping findings.

##### 2. Biochemical, Molecular & Genetic Deep-Dive (生化代谢、基因靶点与分子机制深度剖析)
- **G-Banding:** This technique uses trypsin digestion followed by Giemsa staining to create a characteristic pattern of dark (G-positive) and light (G-negative) bands along the chromosome. The pattern is specific for each chromosome and allows for the identification of structural abnormalities like deletions, duplications, inversions, and translocations.
- **Interpretation of Findings:** The patient has a normal chromosome number (46,XX). The key finding is the abnormality in the *banding pattern* of chromosome 21, specifically involving the short arm (p arm) in the 21p11-p13 region. A shorter p arm and indistinct banding in this region strongly suggest a *deletion* of a portion of the short arm of chromosome 21. This constitutes a partial monosomy for the region 21p11-p13.
- **Clinical Significance:** Deletions of chromosome 21p, even partial ones, can be associated with various phenotypes, including developmental delay, congenital anomalies, and recurrent pregnancy loss. The specific region 21p11-p13 contains several genes, and loss of function of genes in this region can disrupt normal embryonic development.
- **Why this is the best fit:** The karyotype directly shows evidence of a deletion on the short arm of chromosome 21, consistent with a partial monosomy. This finding directly explains the observed banding pattern abnormality. Recurrent pregnancy loss is a known potential consequence of such chromosomal abnormalities.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): A Robertsonian translocation involves the fusion of two acrocentric chromosomes (like 13, 14, 15, 21, 22) at the centromere. While translocations can cause recurrent pregnancy loss (e.g., a balanced translocation in the carrier leading to unbalanced gametes), the karyotype description specifically points to a *deletion* on the short arm of chromosome 21, not a fusion with another chromosome. The banding pattern described (shorter p arm, indistinct bands) is characteristic of a deletion, not a translocation.
- (B): A pericentric inversion involves a segment of a chromosome being inverted (flipped 180 degrees) around the centromere. While inversions can disrupt genes at the breakpoints or affect fertility, the karyotype finding described is a deletion (shorter arm, indistinct bands), not an inversion. Inversions typically show rearranged bands but not necessarily a shortened arm unless the breakpoints are very close to the ends.
- (D): A duplication of the long arm of chromosome 21 (21q) would lead to a phenotype resembling Down syndrome (trisomy 21), but the karyotype would show an extra copy of the long arm or a significantly enlarged long arm, not a normal-appearing long arm with a defect on the short arm. The patient's karyotype shows a normal chromosome number and a normal-appearing long arm of chromosome 21.
- (E): Mosaicism for trisomy 21 (46,XX/47,XX,+21) means the patient has two cell lines: one normal (46,XX) and one with an extra chromosome 21 (47,XX,+21). While mosaic trisomy 21 can cause recurrent pregnancy loss, it would typically be detected as an abnormal chromosome number in the karyotype analysis. The described karyotype is 46,XX, indicating a normal chromosome number in the cells analyzed from the peripheral blood. Furthermore, the specific finding is a banding pattern abnormality on chromosome 21p, not an extra chromosome 21.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Classical G-banded karyotyping is a fundamental cytogenetic technique used to visualize chromosome structure and number. Key features include PHA stimulation for T-cell mitosis, colchicine arrest in metaphase, and Giemsa staining after trypsin treatment. Interpretation requires recognizing normal banding patterns and identifying abnormalities like deletions (loss of bands, shorter arm), duplications (extra bands, longer arm), inversions (rearranged bands), and translocations (fusion of chromosomes or exchange of segments). Recurrent pregnancy loss is often associated with parental chromosomal abnormalities. Differential diagnoses for recurrent pregnancy loss include parental translocations, aneuploidy (like trisomy 21), and structural abnormalities like deletions or inversions. This case highlights the importance of carefully examining banding patterns, even when the chromosome number appears normal.
